<div class='question question'>

Work out $3832 \div 8$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 3832 \div 8 \\\\
&= \dfrac{3832}{8} \\\\
&= \dfrac{479 \times 8}{8} \\\\
&= \dfrac{479 \times \cancel 8}{\cancel 8} \\\\
&= 479
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2:1:5</p>

<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
talgebra
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

$11$ and $12$ are consecutive whole numbers.

Write down two consecutive whole numbers which add up to $75$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


Let the number $=a$

Other number $=a+1$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 1 &=  75 \\\\
2a +1 &=  75 \\\\
2a &= 75 - 1 \\\\
2a &= 74 \\\\
a  &= \dfrac{74}{2} \\\\
a  &= \dfrac{37 \times 2}{2} \\\\
a  &= \dfrac{37 \times \cancel    2}{\cancel 2} \\\\
a  &= 37
\end{aligned}
$

Two consecutive numbers $= 37$ and $38$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$37, 38$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
Let the number $=a$
<emptyline>
Other number $=a+1$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

a  &= \dfrac{37 \times 2}{2} \\\\
a  &= \dfrac{37 \times \cancel    2}{\cancel 2} \\\\
a  &= 37
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Two consecutive numbers $= 37$ and $38$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$37, 38$
</code></pre>

<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out one fifth of $180$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&=  \dfrac {1} {5} \text{ of } 180 \\\\
&=  \dfrac {1} {5} \times 180 \\\\
&=  \dfrac {180} {5} \\\\
&=  \dfrac {36 \times 5} {5} \\\\
&=  \dfrac {36 \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
&=  36
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>

$36$

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Work out $\dfrac{5}{8}$ of $96$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&=  \dfrac {5} {8} \ \text {of} \ 96 \\\\
&=  \dfrac {5} {8} \times 96 \\\\
&=  \dfrac {5 \times 96} {8} \\\\
&=  \dfrac {5 \times 12 \times 8} {8} \\\\
&=  \dfrac {5 \times 12 \times \cancel {8}} {\cancel {8}} \\\\
&=  5 \times 12 \\\\
&=  60
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&=  \dfrac {5} {8} \ \text {of} \ 96 \\\\
&=  \dfrac {5} {8} \times 96 \\\\
&=  \dfrac {5 \times 96} {8} \\\\
&=  \dfrac {5 \times 12 \times 8} {8} \\\\
&=  \dfrac {5 \times 12 \times \cancel {8}} {\cancel {8}} \\\\
&=  5 \times 12 \\\\
&=  60
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$60$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2:1:10</p>

</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Farmer Andrew's chickens lay a total of $314$ eggs.

Each egg box holds only $6$ eggs.

How many egg boxes will Farmer Andrew be able to completely fill with these eggs?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of boxes that can be filled $=a$

As per the question,


$
\begin{aligned}
a                                        &= \dfrac {314} {6} \\\\
a                                        &= \dfrac {157 \times 2} {2 \times 3} \\\\
a                                        &= \dfrac {157 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2 \times 3} \\\\
a                                        &= \dfrac {157} {3} \\\\
a                                        &= 52 \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
\end{aligned}
$

Number of boxes that can be completely filled $= 52$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$52$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of boxes that can be filled $=a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>

$
\begin{aligned}
a                                        &= \dfrac {314} {6} \\\\
a                                        &= \dfrac {157 \times 2} {2 \times 3} \\\\
a                                        &= \dfrac {157 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2 \times 3} \\\\
a                                        &= \dfrac {157} {3} \\\\
a                                        &= 52 \dfrac {1} {3} \\\\
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of boxes that can be completely filled $= 52$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$52$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$3 \times 6 + \boxed{{\phantom{11}}} = 25$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
3 \times 6 + a  &= 25 \\\\
18 + a          &= 25 \\\\
a               &= 25 - 18 \\\\
a               &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$88 \div 4 = \boxed{{\phantom{11}}} - 9$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
88 \div 4                                      &= a - 9 \\\\
\dfrac {88} {4}                                &= a - 9 \\\\
a - 9                                          &= \dfrac {88} {4} \\\\
a - 9                                          &= \dfrac {22 \times 4} {4}  \\\\
a - 9                                          &= \dfrac {22 \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
a - 9                                          &= 22 \\\\
a                                              &= 22 + 9 \\\\
a                                              &= 31
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$31$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the missing number $= a$

a - 9                                          &= \dfrac {88} {4} \\\\
a - 9                                          &= \dfrac {22 \times 4} {4}  \\\\
a - 9                                          &= \dfrac {22 \times \cancel {4}} {\cancel {4}} \\\\
a - 9                                          &= 22 \\\\
a                                              &= 22 + 9 \\\\
a                                              &= 31
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$7^2 =5 1 - (\boxed{{\phantom{11}}} \times 4)$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
7 ^ 2            &= 51 - (a \times 4) \\\\
7 \times 7       &= 51 - 4 a \\\\
49               &= 51 - 4 a \\\\
4 a              &= 51 - 49 \\\\
4 a              &= 2 \\\\
a                &= \dfrac {2} {4} \\\\
a                &= \dfrac {2} {2 \times 2} \\\\
a                &= \dfrac {\cancel 2} {2 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
a                &= \dfrac {1} {2}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\dfrac {1} {2}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the missing number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
7 ^ 2            &= 51 - (a \times 4) \\\\
7 \times 7       &= 51 - 4 a \\\\
49               &= 51 - 4 a \\\\
4 a              &= 51 - 49 \\\\
4 a              &= 2 \\\\
a                &= \dfrac {2} {4} \\\\
a                &= \dfrac {2} {2 \times 2} \\\\
a                &= \dfrac {\cancel 2} {2 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
a                &= \dfrac {1} {2}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\dfrac {1} {2}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

                                &= \dfrac {75} {100} \times 4 \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {75 \times 4} {100} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {25 \times 3 \times 4} {25 \times 4} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel{25} \times 3 \times \cancel{4}} {\cancel{25} \times \cancel{4}} \\\\
                                &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question question'>

The composer Johann Sebastian Bach lived for $65$ years and $4$ months.

The composer Georg Philipp Telemann lived for $86$ years and $3$ months.

For how much longer did Telemann live than Bach?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Difference}      &= \text {Years Telemann lived} - \text {Years Bach lived} \\\\
                        &= 86 \ \text {years} \ 3 \ \text {months} - 65 \ \text {years} \ 4 \ \text {months} \\\\
                        &= 20 \ \text {years} \ 11 \ \text {months}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20 \ \text {years} \ 11 \ \text {months}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Difference}      &= \text {Years Telemann lived} - \text {Years Bach lived} \\\\
                        &= 86 \ \text {years} \ 3 \ \text {months} - 65 \ \text {years} \ 4 \ \text {months} \\\\
                        &= 20 \ \text {years} \ 11 \ \text {months}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$20 \ \text {years} \ 11 \ \text {months}$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

$180 \ \text{grams}$ of blueberries, in addition to other ingredients. 

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Charlotte wants to make just $9$ muffins.

How many grams of each of the following ingredients will she need?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subsubquestion lower-roman question_type_subsubquestion list_type_lower-roman'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

flour

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of flour needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
12 \ \text{muffins} : 300 \ \text{grams}            &= 9 \ \text{muffins} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{12} {300}                                &= \dfrac {9} {a} \\\\
12 \times a                                     &= 9 \times 300 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 300} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 12 \times 25} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel {12} \times 25} {\cancel {12}} \\\\
a                                               &= 9 \times 25 \\\\
a                                               &= 225 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Flour needed $= 225 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{muffins}                          &:& \text{flour} \\\\
= & 12                                      &:& 300 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {300} {12} \\\\
= & 1 \times 9                                       &:& \dfrac {300 \times 9} {12} \\\\
= & 9                                       &:& \dfrac {12 \times 25 \times 9} {12} \\\\
= & 9                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel {12} \times 25 \times 9} {\cancel {12}} \\\\
= & 9                                       &:& 25 \times 9 \\\\
= & 9                                       &:& 225 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Flour needed $= 225 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$225 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

blueberries

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the amount of blueberries needed $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
12 \ \text{muffins} : 180 \ \text{grams}            &= 9 \ \text{muffins} : a \ \text{grams} \\\\
\dfrac{12} {180}                                &= \dfrac {9} {a} \\\\
12 \times a                                     &= 9 \times 180 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 180} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times 12 \times 15} {12} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {9 \times \cancel {12} \times 15} {\cancel {12}} \\\\
a                                               &= 9 \times 15 \\\\
a                                               &= 135 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Blueberries needed $= 135 \ \text{g}$

</div>

$

= & 9                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel {12} \times 15 \times 9} {\cancel {12}} \\\\
= & 9                                       &:& 15 \times 9 \\\\
= & 9                                       &:& 135 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Blueberries needed $= 135 \ \text{g}$

</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Paul has a one kilogram bag of flour. If he uses the whole kilogram of flour to make blueberry muffins, how many can he make?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of muffins he can make $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
300 \ \text{g} : 12 \ \text{muffins}            &= 1 \ \text{kg} : a \ \text{muffins} \\\\
\dfrac{300 \ \text{g}} {12}                     &= \dfrac {1 \ \text{kg}} {a} \\\\
\dfrac{300 \ \text{g}} {12}                     &= \dfrac {1000 \ \text{g}} {a} \\\\
300 \times a                                    &= 1000 \times 12 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {1000 \times 12} {300} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {1000 \times 3 \times 4} {3 \times 100} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {10 \cancel 0 \cancel 0 \times \cancel 3 \times 4} {\cancel 3 \times 1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
a                                               &= 10 \times 4 \\\\
a                                               &= 40
\end{aligned}
$

Number of muffins $= 40$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{flour}                            &:& \text{muffins} \\\\
= & 300 \ \text{g}                          &:& 12 \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{g}                            &:& \dfrac {12} {300} \\\\
= & 1 \times 1000 \ \text{g}                &:& \dfrac {12 \times 1000} {300} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{kg}                           &:& \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 10 \times 100} {3 \times 100} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 4 \times 10 \times \cancel {100}} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel {100}} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& 4 \times 10 \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{kg}                           &:& 40
\end{array}
$

Number of muffins $= 40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of muffins he can make $= a$
<emptyline>
Using equivalent ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
300 \ \text{g} : 12 \ \text{muffins}            &= 1 \ \text{kg} : a \ \text{muffins} \\\\

\dfrac{300 \ \text{g}} {12}                     &= \dfrac {1000 \ \text{g}} {a} \\\\
300 \times a                                    &= 1000 \times 12 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {1000 \times 12} {300} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {1000 \times 3 \times 4} {3 \times 100} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {10 \cancel 0 \cancel 0 \times \cancel 3 \times 4} {\cancel 3 \times 1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
a                                               &= 10 \times 4 \\\\
a                                               &= 40
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of muffins $= 40$
</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{flour}                            &:& \text{muffins} \\\\
= & 300 \ \text{g}                          &:& 12 \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{g}                            &:& \dfrac {12} {300} \\\\
= & 1 \times 1000 \ \text{g}                &:& \dfrac {12 \times 1000} {300} \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{kg}                           &:& \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 10 \times 100} {3 \times 100} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 4 \times 10 \times \cancel {100}} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel {100}} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& 4 \times 10 \\\\
= & 1 \ \text{kg}                           &:& 40
\end{array}
$
<emptyline>
Number of muffins $= 40$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The number machine below changes numbers according to the rule:

multiply by $3$, then add $6$

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-17-subquestion-1-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-17-subquestion-1-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-17-subquestion-1-00.png")

Complete the input and output numbers for the machine.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-17-subquestion-1-01.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-17-subquestion-1-01.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-17-subquestion-1-01.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let output $= a$

$
\begin{aligned}
& 40 \times 3 + 6 &= a \\\\
& 120 + 6 &= a \\\\
& a &= 126
\end{aligned}
$

Let the input $= b$

$
\begin{aligned}
& b \times 3 + 6 &= 54 \\\\
& 3b + 6 &= 54 \\\\
& 3b &= 54 - 6 \\\\
& 3b &= 48 \\\\
& b &= \dfrac{48}{3} \\\\
& b &= 16
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working placeholder'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$126 \\\\ 16$

</div>
<div class='answer placeholder'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

There are two possible function machines which will give results shown below. 

Work out both possible answers.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-17-subquestion-2-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-17-subquestion-2-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-17-subquestion-2-00.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let first label $= \times a$

Let second label $= + b$

$
\begin{aligned}
& a \times 6 + b &= 12 \\\\
& 6a + b &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
& a \times 8 + b &= 20 \\\\
& 8a + b &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'a\'

$
\begin{aligned}
& 8a + b &= 20 \\\\
& 8a + (12 - 6a) &= 20 \\\\
& 8a - 6a &= 20 - 12 \\\\
& 2a &= 8 \\\\
& a &= \dfrac{8}{2} \\\\
& a &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'b\'

$
\begin{aligned}
& 6a + b &= 12 \\\\
& 6(4) + b &= 12 \\\\
& 24 + b &= 12 \\\\
& b &= 12 - 24 \\\\
& b &= - 12
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore, the labels $= \times 4, -12$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Second possible labels

Let first label $= + a$

Let second label $= \times b$

$
\begin{aligned}
(6 + a) \times b &= 12 \\\\
6b + ab &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
(8 + a) \times b &= 20 \\\\
8b + ab &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'b\'

$
\begin{aligned}
8b + (12 - 6b) &= 20 \\\\
8b - 6b &= 20 - 12 \\\\
2b &= 8 \\\\
b &= \dfrac{8}{2} \\\\
b &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

Lets solve for \'a\'

$
\begin{aligned}
(6 + a) \times 4 &= 12 \\\\
6 \times 4 + a \times 4 &= 12 \\\\
24 + 4a &= 12 \\\\
4a &= 12 - 24 \\\\
4a &= -12 \\\\
a &= \dfrac{-12}{4} \\\\
a &= -3
\end{aligned}
$

Therefore, the labels $= + 4, \times -3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\times 4, - 12$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

$+ 4, \times -3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

A packet of $9$ chocolate cookies costs $\pounds 2.61$

What is the cost of $4$ of these chocolate cookies?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$\text {Let the cost of 1 chocolate cookie} = \text{a} $

$\text {As per the question,} $

$
\begin{aligned}
a                                        &= \dfrac {2.61} {9} \\\\
a                                        &= \pounds 0.29 \\\\
\text {Cost of 4 chocolate cookies}      &= a \times 4 \\\\
                                         &= \pounds 1.16
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 1.16$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\text {Let the cost of 1 chocolate cookie} = \text{a} $

                                         &= \pounds 1.16
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Carla bought 2 books from a shop, and paid a total of $\pounds 8.50$

One book cost $60 \ \text{pence}$ more than the other.

What was the price of the cheaper book?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required
. Let's convert all the units into $\pounds$.

Let the cost of one book $= a$

Cost of second book $= a + 60 \ \text{p}$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 60 \ \text{p}             &= \pounds 8.50 \\\\
2 a + \pounds \dfrac {60} {100}    &= \pounds 8.50 \\\\
2 a + \pounds 0.60                 &= \pounds 8.50 \\\\
2 a                                &= 8.50 - 0.60 \\\\
2 a                                &= 7.90 \\\\
a                                  &= \dfrac {7.90} {2} \\\\
a                                  &= \pounds 3.95
\end{aligned}
$

Cost of cheaper book $= \pounds 3.95$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 3.95$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_no_dot_note. Let's convert all the units into $\pounds$.
<emptyline>
Let the cost of one book $= a$
<emptyline>
Cost of second book $= a + 60 \ \text{p}$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + a + 60 \ \text{p}             &= \pounds 8.50 \\\\
2 a + \pounds \dfrac {60} {100}    &= \pounds 8.50 \\\\
2 a + \pounds 0.60                 &= \pounds 8.50 \\\\
2 a                                &= 8.50 - 0.60 \\\\
2 a                                &= 7.90 \\\\
a                                  &= \dfrac {7.90} {2} \\\\
a                                  &= \pounds 3.95
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Cost of cheaper book $= \pounds 3.95$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 3.95$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}

                          &= 7.0
\end{aligned}
$

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

In a number sequence, the $2 \text{nd}$ term is $6$ and the $5 ^\text{th}$ term is $18$, as shown below.

What is the $7 ^\text{th}$ term in the sequence?

$\ldots, \quad 6, \quad \ldots, \quad \ldots, \quad 18, \quad \ldots, \quad \ldots$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the difference $= d$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
6 + 3d                        &= 18 \\\\
3d                            &= 18 - 6 \\\\
3d                            &= 12 \\\\
d                             &= \dfrac{12}{3} \\\\
d                             &= \dfrac{3 \times 4}{3} \\\\
d                             &= \dfrac{\cancel{3} \times 4}{\cancel{3}} \\\\
d                             &= 4 \\\\
7 ^ \text{th} \text{ term}    &= 18 + 2d \\\\
                              &= 18 + 2 \times 4 \\\\
                              &= 18 + 8 \\\\
                              &= 26
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$26$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the difference $= d$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
6 + 3d                        &= 18 \\\\
3d                            &= 18 - 6 \\\\
3d                            &= 12 \\\\
d                             &= \dfrac{12}{3} \\\\
d                             &= \dfrac{3 \times 4}{3} \\\\
d                             &= \dfrac{\cancel{3} \times 4}{\cancel{3}} \\\\
d                             &= 4 \\\\
7 ^ \text{th} \text{ term}    &= 18 + 2d \\\\
                              &= 18 + 2 \times 4 \\\\
                              &= 18 + 8 \\\\
                              &= 26
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$26$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2:1:21</p>

<div class='question question'>

Mr Cooper's car uses $3 \ \text{litres}$ of petrol to travel $50 \ \text{kilometres}$.

Petrol costs $\pounds 1.45$ per litre.

How much will Mr Cooper spend on petrol to drive $600 \ \text{kilometres}$ from Oxford to Glasgow?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Petrol needed for 1 km}      &= \dfrac {3} {50} \\\\
\text {Petrol needed for 600 km}    &= \dfrac {3} {50} \times 600 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 600} {50} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 50 \times 12} {50} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {50} \times 12} {\cancel {50}} \\\\
                                    &= 3 \times 12 \\\\
                                    &= 36 \ \text{litres} \\\\
\text {Cost of the petrol}          &= 1.45 \times 36 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 52.2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{km}                                     &:& \text{litres} \\\\
= & 50                                            &:& 3 \\\\
= & 1                                             &:& \dfrac {3} {50} \\\\
= & 1 \times 600                                  &:& \dfrac {3 \times 600} {50} \\\\
= & 600                                           &:& \dfrac {3 \times 50 \times 12} {50} \\\\
= & 600                                           &:& \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {50} \times 12} {\cancel {50}} \\\\
= & 600                                           &:& 3 \times 12 \\\\
= & 600 \ \text{km}                               &:& 36 \ \text{litres}
\end{array}
$

<br>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Petrol needed}       &= 36 \ \text{litres} \\\\
\text {Cost of the petrol}  &= 1.45 \times 36 \\\\
                            &= \pounds 52.2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 52.2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Petrol needed for 1 km}      &= \dfrac {3} {50} \\\\
\text {Petrol needed for 600 km}    &= \dfrac {3} {50} \times 600 \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 600} {50} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times 50 \times 12} {50} \\\\
                                    &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel {50} \times 12} {\cancel {50}} \\\\
                                    &= 3 \times 12 \\\\
                                    &= 36 \ \text{litres} \\\\
\text {Cost of the petrol}          &= 1.45 \times 36 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 52.2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using ratio,

$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='answers'>

&= 19{:}15

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subsubquestion'>
<div class='question subsubquestion'>

If each girl was sponsored $10 \ \text{pence}$ for each minute that she remained silent, how much money did the three girls raise in total?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Silent Time}                  &= 16{:}29 - 15{:}00 + 17{:}08 - 15{:}00 + 16{:}23 - 15{:}00 \\\\
                                     &= 1 \ \text {hour} \ 29 \ \text {minutes} + 2 \ \text {hour} \ 8 \ \text {minutes} + 1 \ \text {hour} \ 23 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
                                     &= 1 \times 60 \ \text {minutes} + 29 \ \text {minutes} + 2 \times 60 \ \text {minutes} + 8 \ \text {minutes} + 1 \times 60 \ \text {minutes} + 23 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
                                     &= 60 + 29 + 120 + 8 + 60 + 23 \\\\
                                     &= 300 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
\text {Total money raised}           &= 300 \times 10 \ \text {pence} \\\\
                                     &= 3000 \ \text {pence}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3000 \ \text {pence}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Silent Time}                  &= 16{:}29 - 15{:}00 + 17{:}08 - 15{:}00 + 16{:}23 - 15{:}00 \\\\
                                     &= 1 \ \text {hour} \ 29 \ \text {minutes} + 2 \ \text {hour} \ 8 \ \text {minutes} + 1 \ \text {hour} \ 23 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
                                     &= 1 \times 60 \ \text {minutes} + 29 \ \text {minutes} + 2 \times 60 \ \text {minutes} + 8 \ \text {minutes} + 1 \times 60 \ \text {minutes} + 23 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
                                     &= 60 + 29 + 120 + 8 + 60 + 23 \\\\
                                     &= 300 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
\text {Total money raised}           &= 300 \times 10 \ \text {pence} \\\\
                                     &= 3000 \ \text {pence}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

What percentage of the people said Edam?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{\%  who said Edam}                      &= \dfrac {90} {360} \times 100 \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {90 \times 100} {360} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {90 \times 4 \times 25} {90 \times 4} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {\cancel {90} \times \cancel 4 \times 25} {\cancel {90} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                                              &= 25 \%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$25 \%$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

                                              &= \dfrac {90 \times 4 \times 25} {90 \times 4} \\\\
                                              &= \dfrac {\cancel {90} \times \cancel 4 \times 25} {\cancel {90} \times \cancel 4} \\\\
                                              &= 25 \%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

$18$ people said Edam.

How many people said Goat's Cheese?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the total number of people $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
a \times \dfrac {90} {360}    &= 18 \\\\
a                             &= 18 \times \dfrac {360} {90} \\\\
a                             &= \dfrac {18 \times 360} {90} \\\\
a                             &= \dfrac {18 \times 90 \times 4} {90} \\\\
a                             &= \dfrac {18 \times \cancel {90} \times 4} {\cancel {90}} \\\\
a                             &= 18 \times 4 \\\\
a                             &= 72
\end {aligned}
$

$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Number of people who said Goat's cheese}    &= \dfrac {30} {360} \times 72 \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {30 \times 72} {360} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {5 \times 6 \times 72} {72 \times 5} \\\\
                                                  &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 6 \times \cancel {72}} {\cancel {72} \times \cancel 5} \\\\
                                                  &= 6
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{Edam}                                 &:& \text{Goat's} \\\\
= & 90                                          &:& 30 \\\\
= & \dfrac {90} {5}                             &:& \dfrac {30} {5} \\\\
= & \dfrac {18 \times 5} {5}                    &:& \dfrac {6 \times 5} {5} \\\\
= & \dfrac {18 \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 5}    &:& \dfrac {6 \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 5} \\\\
= & 18                                          &:& 6
\end{array}
$

Number of people who said Goat's cheese $= 6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the mean (average) cost of a dark chocolate?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Mean cost}   &= \dfrac {145} {5} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {5 \times 29} {5} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 29} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                    &= 29 \ \text{p}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$29 \ \text{p}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Mean cost}   &= \dfrac {28 - 2 + 35 - 2 + 27 - 2 + 25 - 2 + 30 - 2} {5} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {145 - 10} {5} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {135} {5} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 27} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                    &= 27 \ \text{p}
\end {aligned}
$

The point $E$ is such that if a line is drawn from $B$ to $E$, point $C$ is two thirds of the way from $B$ to $E$.

Write down the coordinates of point $E$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing starts.*

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)



Plot the line joining B and C and extend the line BC to E such that length of BC is double of length of CE


*ABBR: TODO:: Add drawing ends.*


Let the coordinates of E $= (a, b)$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
\dfrac {2a + 7} {2 + 1}       &= 9 \\\\
\dfrac {2a + 7} {3}           &= 9 \\\\
2a + 7                        &= 9 \times 3 \\\\
2a + 7                        &= 27 \\\\
2a                            &= 27 - 7 \\\\
2a                            &= 20 \\\\
a                             &= \dfrac {20} {2} \\\\
a                             &= \dfrac {2 \times 10} {2} \\\\
a                             &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 10} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                             &= 10 \\\\
\dfrac {2b + 8} {2 + 1}       &= 4 \\\\
\dfrac {2b + 8} {3}           &= 4 \\\\
2b + 8                        &= 4 \times 3 \\\\
2b + 8                        &= 12 \\\\
2b                            &= 12 - 8 \\\\
2b                            &= 4 \\\\
b                             &= \dfrac {4} {2} \\\\
b                             &= \dfrac {2 \times 2} {2} \\\\
b                             &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
b                             &= 2
\end {aligned}
$

Coordinates $= (10, 2)$

</div>

<div class='answer'>

$(10, 2)$

<div class='question question'>

Field crickets chirp according to the temperature of their surroundings.

You can use the following rule to work out the temperature:

$\text{temperature in} \degrees \text{C} = \text{(number of chirps per minute} - 40 \text{)} \div 7 + 10$

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Freddie, the field cricket, chirps $61 \text{times per minute}$.

Work out the temperature of Freddie's surroundings.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Freddie's surroundings temperature}   &= (61 - 40) \div 7 + 10 \\\\
                                            &= 21 \div 7 + 10 \\\\
                                            &= 3 + 10 \\\\
                                            &= 13 \degree \text{C}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$13 \degree \text{C}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}

                                            &= 21 \div 7 + 10 \\\\
                                            &= 3 + 10 \\\\
                                            &= 13 \degree \text{C}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Flora, the field cricket, chirps $41$ times in $30 \ \text{seconds}$.

Work out the temperature of Flora's surroundings.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Flora's surroundings temperature}     &= (41 \times 2 - 40) \div 7 + 10 \\\\
                                            &= (82 - 40) \div 7 + 10 \\\\
                                            &= 42 \div 7 + 10 \\\\
                                            &= 6 + 10 \\\\
                                            &= 16 \degree \text{C}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$16 \degree \text{C}$

</div>
</div>

                                            &= (82 - 40) \div 7 + 10 \\\\
                                            &= 42 \div 7 + 10 \\\\
                                            &= 6 + 10 \\\\
                                            &= 16 \degree \text{C}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The temperature of Flossie's surroundings is $18 \degree \text{C}$.

Work out how many times Flossie chirps in $1 \ \text{minute}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let Flossie chirps (in $1 \ \text{minute}$) $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
18                          &= (a - 40) \div 7 + 10 \\\\
18                          &= \dfrac {a - 40} {7} + 10 \\\\
\dfrac {a - 40} {7} + 10    &= 18 \\\\
\dfrac {a - 40} {7}         &= 18 - 10 \\\\
\dfrac {a - 40} {7}         &= 8 \\\\
a                           &= 8 \times 7 + 40 \\\\
a                           &= 56 + 40 \\\\
a                           &= 96
\end{aligned}
$

Flossie chirps (in $1 \ \text{minute}$) $= 96$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$96$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

\dfrac {a - 40} {7}         &= 8 \\\\

a                           &= 56 + 40 \\\\
a                           &= 96
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Flossie chirps (in $1 \ \text{minute}$) $= 96$
</code></pre>
</div>

$
\begin {aligned}

                        &= 1 \times 60  \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                        &= 60 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
\text {Angle moved}     &= 60 \times 0.5 \\\\
                        &= 30 \degree
\end {aligned}

<div class='question_envelope rag_g1 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>376F37EC-1A3D-4857-B11B-3A35720D6E40</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2:1:33</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
talgebra
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Jane bought a packet of sweets.

A quarter of them were green, a third of them were red and the rest were yellow.

There were $10$ yellow sweets.

What was the total number of sweets in the packet?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of sweets $= a$

Number of green sweets $= \dfrac{a}{4}$

Number of red sweets $= \dfrac{a}{3}$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a} {4} + \dfrac {a} {3} + 10                                                                        &= a \\\\
a - \dfrac {a} {4} - \dfrac {a} {3}                                                                         &= 10 \\\\
\dfrac {a \times 12} {1 \times 12} - \dfrac {a \times 3} {4 \times 3} - \dfrac {a \times 4} {3 \times 4}    &= 10 \\\\
\dfrac {12a} {12} - \dfrac {3a} {12} - \dfrac {4a} {12}                                                     &= 10 \\\\
\dfrac {12a - 3a - 4a} {12}                                                                                 &= 10 \\\\
\dfrac{5a}{12}                                                                                              &= 10 \\\\
5a                                                                                                          &= 10 \times 12 \\\\
a                                                                                                           &= \dfrac {10 \times 12} {5} \\\\
a                                                                                                           &= \dfrac {5 \times 2 \times 12} {5} \\\\
a                                                                                                           &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 2 \times 12} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
a                                                                                                           &= 2 \times 12 \\\\
a                                                                                                           &= 24
\end{aligned}
$

Total number of sweets $=24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of sweets $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a} {4} + \dfrac {a} {3} + 10                                                                        &= a \\\\
a - \dfrac {a} {4} - \dfrac {a} {3}                                                                         &= 10 \\\\
\dfrac {a \times 12} {1 \times 12} - \dfrac {a \times 3} {4 \times 3} - \dfrac {a \times 4} {3 \times 4}    &= 10 \\\\
\dfrac {12a} {12} - \dfrac {3a} {12} - \dfrac {4a} {12}                                                     &= 10 \\\\
\dfrac {12a - 3a - 4a} {12}                                                                                 &= 10 \\\\
\dfrac{5a}{12}                                                                                              &= 10 \\\\
5a                                                                                                          &= 10 \times 12 \\\\
a                                                                                                           &= \dfrac {10 \times 12} {5} \\\\
a                                                                                                           &= \dfrac {5 \times 2 \times 12} {5} \\\\
a                                                                                                           &= \dfrac {\cancel {5} \times 2 \times 12} {\cancel {5}} \\\\
a                                                                                                           &= 2 \times 12 \\\\
a                                                                                                           &= 24
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Total number of sweets $=24$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$24$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p>6DD89262-C913-4868-9BD3-AC3F1EF2710A</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2:1:35</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g3</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tpermutation
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

In a doctor's waiting room, there are $6$ seats in a row.

Mr Spencer arrives with his $2$ children.

He wants to sit between his $2$ children.

$3$ other people arrive who do not mind where they sit.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-35-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-35-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-35-00.png")

How many different possible seating arrangements are there of the $6$ people?

*You may find the boxes helpful*.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-35-01.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-35-01.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-35-01.png")

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let Mr Spencer $= \text{S}$

Let his children $= \text{C1}$ and $\text{C2}$

Let the other three people $= \text{P1, P2, P3}$

If Mr Spencer sits in the second seat, the possible arrangements are:

- $\text{C1  \quad S  \quad C2 \quad P1 \quad P2 \quad P3}$
- $\text{C1  \quad S  \quad C2 \quad P1 \quad P3 \quad P2}$
- $\text{C1  \quad S  \quad C2 \quad P2 \quad P1 \quad P3}$
- $\text{C1  \quad S  \quad C2 \quad P2 \quad P3 \quad P1}$
- $\text{C1  \quad S  \quad C2 \quad P3 \quad P1 \quad P2}$
- $\text{C1  \quad S  \quad C2 \quad P3 \quad P2 \quad P1}$
- $\text{C2  \quad S  \quad C1 \quad P1 \quad P2 \quad P3}$
- $\text{C2  \quad S  \quad C1 \quad P1 \quad P3 \quad P2}$
- $\text{C2  \quad S  \quad C1 \quad P2 \quad P1 \quad P3}$
- $\text{C2  \quad S  \quad C1 \quad P2 \quad P3 \quad P1}$
- $\text{C2  \quad S  \quad C1 \quad P3 \quad P1 \quad P2}$
- $\text{C2  \quad S  \quad C1 \quad P3 \quad P2 \quad P1}$

Count $= 12$

If Mr Spencer sits in the third seat, the possible arrangements are:

- $\text{P1 \quad C1 \quad S \quad C2 \quad P2 \quad P3}$
- $\text{P1 \quad C1 \quad S \quad C2 \quad P3 \quad P2}$
- $\text{P2 \quad C1 \quad S \quad C2 \quad P1 \quad P3}$

<div class='working'>

Using permutation,

Since Mr Spencer has to sit between his two children, he can only sit in the middle four seats.

Number of places Mr Spencer can sit $= 4$

Number of places his first child can sit $= 2$

Number of places his second child can sit $= 1$

Number of ways the fourth seat can be occupied $= 3$

Number of ways the fifth seat can be occupied $= 2$

Number of ways the sixth seat can be occupied $= 1$

Total arrangements $= 4 \times 2 \times 1 \times 3 \times 2 \times 1 = 48$

</div>

<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$48$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The shape below is made from squares.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-36-subquestion-1-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-36-subquestion-1-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-36-subquestion-1-00.png")

The perimeter of the shape is $100 \ \text{centimetres}$.

Work out the area of the shape.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of polygon} = \text{Sum of all sides}$


Let the side of the square $= s$

Number of sides in the shape $= 20$

Number of squares $= 10$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
20 \times s         &= 100 \\\\
20s                 &= 100 \\\\
s                   &= \dfrac {100} {20} \\\\
s                   &= \dfrac {20 \times 5} {20} \\\\
s                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times 5} {\cancel {20}} \\\\
s                   &= 5 \ \text {cm} \\\\
\text {Total Area}  &= 10 \times \text {Area of a square} \\\\
                    &= 10 \times 5 \times 5 \\\\
                    &= 250 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$250 \ \text {cm}^2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_polygon

s                   &= 5 \ \text {cm} \\\\
\text {Total Area}  &= 10 \times \text {Area of a square} \\\\
                    &= 10 \times 5 \times 5 \\\\
                    &= 250 \ \text {cm}^2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</code></pre>

$

<div class='question question'>

The time in Sri Lanka is $4 \dfrac{1}{2} \ \text{hours}$ ahead of London.

For example, when it is $3 \ \text{p.m.}$ in London, it is $7.30 \ \text{p.m.}$ in Sri Lanka.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What time is it in London when it is $1.15 \ \text{p.m.}$ in Sri Lanka?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time in London}        &= 1{:}15 - 4 \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text {hours} \\\\
                             &= 1{:}15 - 4 \ \text {hours} \ 30 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
                             &= 8{:}45 \ \text {am}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8{:}45 \ \text {am}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Time in London}        &= 1{:}15 - 4 \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text {hours} \\\\
                             &= 1{:}15 - 4 \ \text {hours} \ 30 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
                             &= 8{:}45 \ \text {am}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8{:}45 \ \text {am}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The time in New York is $5 \ \text{hours}$ behind the time in London.

Bill's journey from New York to Sri Lanka takes exactly $30 \ \text{hours}$.

Bill leaves New York on Wednesday at $19{:}40$

On which day and at what time does Bill arrive in Sri Lanka?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Time in Sri Lanka}  &= 19{:}40 + 30 \ \text{hours} +  4 \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text{hours}+ 5 \ \text{hours} \\\\
                           &= 19{:}40 + 30 \ \text{hours} +   4 \ \text{hours} \ 30 \ \text{minutes} + 5 \ \text{hours} \\\\
                           &= 19{:}40 + 30 \ \text{hours} +   4 \ \text{hours} + 30 \ \text{minutes} + 5 \ \text{hours} \\\\
                           &= 19{:}40 + 39 \ \text{hours} +   30 \ \text{minutes}  \\\\
                           &= 19{:}40 + 1 \ \text{day} + 15 \ \text{hours} +   30 \ \text{minutes}  \\\\
                           &= 19{:}40  + 15 \ \text{hours} \ 30 \ \text{minutes}  \\\\
                           &= 11{:}10
\end{aligned}
$

Day Bill arrived Sri Lanka $= \text {Friday}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11{:}10$

</div>
<div class='answer'>

Friday

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Time in Sri Lanka}  &= 19{:}40 + 30 \ \text{hours} +  4 \dfrac {1} {2} \ \text{hours}+ 5 \ \text{hours} \\\\
                           &= 19{:}40 + 30 \ \text{hours} +   4 \ \text{hours} \ 30 \ \text{minutes} + 5 \ \text{hours} \\\\
                           &= 19{:}40 + 30 \ \text{hours} +   4 \ \text{hours} + 30 \ \text{minutes} + 5 \ \text{hours} \\\\
                           &= 19{:}40 + 39 \ \text{hours} +   30 \ \text{minutes}  \\\\
                           &= 19{:}40 + 1 \ \text{day} + 15 \ \text{hours} +   30 \ \text{minutes}  \\\\
                           &= 19{:}40  + 15 \ \text{hours} \ 30 \ \text{minutes}  \\\\
                           &= 11{:}10
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Day Bill arrived Sri Lanka $= \text {Friday}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$11{:}10$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Friday
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='question question'>

On a game show, there are $3$ doors.

Behind one door, there is $\pounds 1000$ and behind another, there is a spider!

Behind the other door there is nothing.

Each door has a notice. However, only one notice is true.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-41-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-41-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2014--group-2/section-1-question-41-00.png")
